Let's design specific algorithms to implement robustly:

A. Analytic initializers:
- Taylor coefficients up to n: using recurrence a[0]=1; for k in 1..n-1: a[k] = (1 - np.dot(a[1:k], a[k-1:0:-1])) / 2? Actually sum_{i=1}^{k-1} a_i a_{k-i}. For Taylor target c=1. This O(n^2) but do once for n up to 8192? 67M operations, maybe okay with numpy? Python loop 8192 with dot average 4096 -> 33M, okay maybe seconds. For many n choose only one. But we can generate central binomial coefficients directly via math.comb? For k up to n, a_k = comb(2k,k)/4^k. Use recurrence a_{k+1} = a_k * (2k+1)/(2k+2)? Let's check central binomial ratio: C(2k+2,k+1)/C(2k,k) = (2k+2)(2k+1)/(k+1)^2 = 2(2k+1)/(k+1). Divide by 4: ratio=(2k+1)/(2(k+1)). Yes. So Taylor a[k+1]=a[k]*(2k+1)/(2*(k+1)), starting a0=1. O(n) vectorized! Great.
- Power: a[i]=(i+1)^-β.
- Exponential: exp(-γ i/(n-1)).
- Linear/sine: 1 + t * phi_i, phi decreasing from -1 to 1? Ensure >0.
- Block step: create levels via sort.

B. Local search methods:
1. Coordinate stochastic local search (SLS) with exact score:

Actually np.convolve direct maybe O(n^2) (it may use direct for small, FFT? Not sure). Calling evaluate_sequence many times with length 8192 could be slow due np.convolve in C but maybe still okay? np.convolve for 8192 length full uses direct C? It might use ravel and convolution; could be O(n^2) ~67M, per eval too slow. For our internal evaluations, we can compute score faster with FFT. But final we can call evaluate_sequence once or few. For local search, implement our own fast score using FFT or direct for small n. Must ensure exact same objective; scale independent. We can compute c = fft convolve (zero-pad) and max. For nonnegative small, FFT good. For n small, np.convolve okay.
   - Since evaluate_sequence clips negatives and caps; our arrays nonnegative, finite, sum>0. Our fast score: if sum<0.01 return inf; else 2*n*max(c)/sum^2.
   - For speed, normalize sum=1 before convolve? If sum fixed=1, c smaller; score=2n*max(c). This avoids large numbers. But changes scale; objective invariant. Good.

2. Perturbation moves:
   - Multiplicative block scaling: candidate = a.copy; choose random block [l,r], multiply by exp(N(0,sigma)), possibly enforce monotonic? Since scale invariant, no need preserve sum; score invariant. But flipping scale? If all multiplied, score same. Changing a block relative to others changes shape. This can fill tail. Use adaptive sigma.
   - Add random smooth trend: candidate = a * exp(ε * q) where q is a random low-frequency cosine/spline. This explores shape space.
   - Coordinate moves: pick random index, multiply by exp(N(0,0.1)).
   - Gradient-based moves: compute gradient of smoothed M or score, propose a' = softmax move.

Let's design a simulated annealing / random restart with fast exact score for n=1024 or 2048.
- Population of K=50 candidates, each with score.
- Initially diverse analyzers.
- Each iteration: select candidate via tournament by score (lower better), generate offspring by random smooth perturbation, update population if better than worst or fitter. Also occasionally restart from best with larger perturbation.
- After time, return best raw list.

For n=2048, population 50, each eval FFT 0.002s, 1500s ~750k evals? Overhead maybe 100k. Good. But random search in 2048 dims may need many. Use structured perturbations:
   - Offspring = parent * exp(σ * S), where S is a random isotropic smooth vector: S = combination of m random cosines with random phases/frequencies (low-pass). This changes whole shape smoothly, keeps positive. For low frequencies, can shift mass from start to end. Also with some local blocks.
- σ schedule based on dimension: σ ~ 0.1 to 1.0. Since score scale invariant, large σ may create extremes but positivity ok if exp within [e^-3,e^3]. Clip to >1e-12.

Let's estimate internal FFT score implementation:
```
def fast_score(a):
    n=a.size
    s=a.sum()
    if s<1e-12: return inf
    a=a/s
    N=1
    while N<2*n-1: N*=2
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa,N)[:2*n-1]
    M=c.max()
    return 2*n*M
```
FFT of real positive; c may have tiny negative due roundoff; max unaffected. For n=2048, N=4096? 2n-1=4095, N=4096. Very fast.
Could precompute N for each n and use. For many eval with same n, avoid recompute transform? Multiplicative perturb changes a, need full FFT each. Use `np.fft.rfft` in numpy uses pocketfft, fast.
For n=4096, N=8192; still okay. For n=8192, N=16384; maybe 0.005ms? Actually 16k FFT ~0.3ms in Python? maybe 1ms. Thousands okay.

We need final returned sequence can be length n optimized. Use list of floats. Ensure all nonnegative, sum >0.01 (scale arbitrary). We can return raw best (not normalized) or normalized? Evaluator scale invariant but clips negatives. Scale doesn't matter. To avoid issues with sum<0.01 if very small, return best normalized to sum=1 (or maybe sum=√(2n) but any >0.01). If normalize sum=1, some entries for large n ~1/n=1e-6, fine. No zeros? If some exp underflow, clip to 1e-15.

def optimize_n(n, time_limit):
    deadline

Need avoid too complex. We can implement a simple population local search:
- Generate initial population of size P (e.g., 20) for n.
  - init_base: Taylor (a=Taylor).
  - init_power: (i+1)^-α for α in [0.5,0.6,0.7,0.8,1.0]? Are α>0.5 maybe worse but include.
  - init_exp: exp(-γ i/(n-1)) for γ [0.5,1,2,3].
  - init_sine: 1+0.8*cos(π i/(n-1))? This high at start low end but positive? cos from1 to -1, 1+0.8cos from1.8 to0.2.
  - init_linear: 1 - 0.8*i/(n-1).
  - init_random smooth: Taylor * exp(0.5*random cosine).
- Compute scores.
- Main loop until deadline:
   - Choose parent: tournament of 3-5 random population members by score.
   - Generate offspring:
      * With probability 0.5: multiplicative smooth low-freq: log_off = log(parent) + σ * (w1*cos(2π f1 i + p1)+w2*cos(...)+... + local block noise). σ adaptive: start 0.3, reduce over time? Use base σ=0.5.
      * With prob 0.3: block multiplicative: random interval length n/(2^k..n/10) multiply by exp(N(0,σ_block)).
      * With prob 0.2: single block coordinate optimization: pick random block (size n/B) and try few multipliers.
   - Evaluate offspring.
   - If score < parent or better than worst, replace worst or parent? Use elitism: keep best separately; population sorted.
   - Every 500 iterations, run a few coordinate refinement passes on best.
- At end return best.

Coordinate multiplicative refinement is deterministic and likely improves. Let's design it well.

Block coordinate search:
Given base array a, choose B blocks equal size (e.g., B=64 or 128). We can update block multipliers. Instead of maintaining multipliers, directly modify a block by scalar factor (reassign a[l:r] *= factor). This changes shape; scale invariant. For each block, find best factor among a set centered at 1: [0.75,0.9,1.1,1.25,1.5] (and maybe 0.5,1.75). Since block size maybe 16-64, modifying all entries by same factor. Evaluate full score. If improvement, accept. Cycle through blocks until no improvement or time. This is O(B * evals) per cycle. For n=2048, B=64, eval 0.002s, one cycle 0.13s, many cycles. Use adaptive factors and random order.
```
def block_refine(a, deadline, B):
    block_size=n//B
    improved=True
    while improved and time<deadline:
       improved=False
       for b in random_cycle:
          best_factor=1
          for factor in [0.7,0.85,1.15,1.3,1.5]:
             a2=a.copy(); a2[l:r]*=factor; s=score_fast(a2)
             if s<best_s: ...
          if best_factor !=1: a=best; improved=True
```
This may get stuck due factor set; include stochastic factor `exp(rng.normal(0,0.2))` occasionally.
Also do per-coordinate local search for best: pick random coordinate, multiply by factors, if improves accept. For n=2048, 2048 coords * few factors = many evals but doable.

Block coordinate can optimize a step profile. Starting from Taylor, it may find better step heights. We can also start from a two-level block optimized by coordinate.

def profile_search(n, B, time_limit):
    v = zeros(B)
    pop = [v + random normal]
    for loop:
       select best, generate offspring v + sigma * random
       evaluate a = baseline * exp(clip v, ±3)

Let's implement parametric initializers for n in a set:
```
def make_sequence(n, kind, params):
  i=np.arange(n)
  if kind=='taylor': O(n) recurrence
  if kind=='power': (i+1)**(-beta)
  if kind=='exp': np.exp(-gamma*i/(n-1))
  if kind=='linear': 1 + tilt * i/(n-1) (ensure positive)
  if kind=='sine': 1 + tilt*np.cos(pi*i/(n-1)) maybe tilt 0.5-0.9
  if kind='block': two-level or multi-level.
```
Normalize sum=1 internally; but for optimization raw shape matters.

Let's implement interpolation:
```
def upscale(arr, n_new):
    x_old=np.arange(len(arr)); x_new=np.linspace(0,n_new-1,n_new)
    # interpolate positive values; use log to keep relative
    y=np.interp(x_new, x_old, arr)
    y=np.clip(y,0,None); if sum=0 fallback taylor
    return y
```
Maybe better use monotone cubic? np.interp linear okay.

def propose_candidate(budget_s=1500):
    start=time.monotonic(); deadline=start+budget_s-20
    best_seq=None; best_score=inf
    # quick parametric scan

Block refine implementation efficient:
```
def block_refine(a, n, deadline, B, seed):
    a=np.array(a,float); s=fast_score(a)
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    block_size = max(1, n//B)
    B = n//block_size
    # factors sorted around 1
    while time < deadline:
        improved=False
        order=rng.permutation(B)
        for b in order:
            l=b*block_size; r=min(n,l+block_size)
            best_s=s; best_f=1.0; best_arr=None
            # try factors
            for f in [0.7,0.85,1.15,1.3,1.5, 1.0/rng?]:
                a2=a.copy(); a2[l:r]*=f
                s2=fast_score(a2)
                if s2<best_s: ...
            # occasionally random factor
            if rng.random()<0.3:
                f=float(np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.3)))
                ...
            if best_f!=1:
                a=best_arr; s=best_s; improved=True
                # optional tighten around best_f with finer ratios
                for f in [1+ (best_f-1)*0.5, ...]? maybe
        if not improved: break
    return a,s
```
Potential issue: Block multipliers can blow up over cycles, causing some blocks huge and others tiny; FFT values normalized so fine. But if a block huge, relative shape step. Accept.

def v_search(n, B, baseline, time_limit, pop_size=30, sigma_init=1.0):
    best_v=zeros(B); best_score=score(baseline)
    pop=[best_v.copy()+rng.normal(0,sigma_init) for _ in range(pop_size)] maybe include 0
    for pop: score
    for iter:
       if time break
       # CMA-like: sigma decreasing based on best count
       # offspring = best_v + sigma * rng.normal(size=B) (or from random parent)
       # also coordinate moves: best_v with one block ±0.2
       eval
       update population (selection)
    return best_v*exp + baseline
```

Implement `fast_score` carefully:
```
def fast_score(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0: return np.inf
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = a.sum()
    if s < 1e-12: return np.inf
    a = a / s
    n = a.size
    if n <= 256:
        c = np.convolve(a, a, mode='full')
        return float(2.0 * n * c.max())
    need = 2*n - 1
    N = 1 << (need - 1).bit_length()
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)[:need]
    # due roundoff, c may have tiny negative, max okay; maybe clip
    M = float(np.max(c))
    if M < 0: M=0.0
    return float(2.0 * n * M)
```
`bit_length` of need-1; for need=4095, (4094).bit_length=12, 1<<12=4096. For need=1, (0).bit_length=0, N=1 okay.
For n=1, convolve [1], score 2.

For v_search, many calls with same n and N; we can cache N in global dict keyed n to avoid bit_length and maybe use `np.fft.rfft` with fixed N. Could not preallocate fa. Fine.

a=np.empty(n); a[0]=1.0
for k in range(n-1):
    a[k+1]=a[k]*(2*k+1)/(2*(k+2?))
```
Check ratio for k -> k+1: a_{k+1}=a_k * (2k+1)/(2(k+1)). For k=0: 1/(2)=0.5. Good. If n=1, [1]. Use for loop; n up to 8192 -> okay. Could vectorize cumulative product: ratios = (2*np.arange(n-1)+1)/(2*(np.arange(n-1)+1)); a[1:]=np.cumprod(ratios). Better.

```

Let's code `block_refine` in more detail:
```
def block_refine(a, deadline, seed, B_list=None):
    a = normalize_array(a)
    s = fast_score(a)
    n = a.size
    if B_list is None:
       if n <= 512: B_list=[8,16,32,64]
       elif n <= 2048: B_list=[16,32,64,128]
       else: B_list=[16,32,64,128,256]
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    total_time = max(1.0, deadline - time.monotonic())
    fracs = len(B_list)
    for idx,B in enumerate(B_list):
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
        # allocate at least 5s if possible
        chunk_loc = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + total_time / fracs * 1.5) #? Maybe simple: loop until no improvement but with deadline; if first B uses all, no later. To limit, set chunk = min(deadline, time+remaining/fracs)
        remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
        if remaining <= 0: break
        chunk_deadline = time.monotonic() + remaining / (len(B_list)-idx)
        block_size = max(1, n // B)
        B = max(1, n // block_size)
        # factors static
        static_factors = [0.6,0.8,0.9,1.1,1.25,1.5]
        improved = True
        while improved and time.monotonic() < chunk_deadline:
            improved = False
            order = rng.permutation(B)
            for b in order:
                if time.monotonic() >= chunk_deadline: break
                l=b*block_size; r=min(n,l+block_size)
                best_local_s=s; best_f=1.0
                # try factors; to save, first try 0.8,1.25 maybe
                for f in (0.7,0.9,1.1,1.3):
                    a2=a.copy(); a2[l:r]*=f
                    s2=fast_score(a2)
                    if s2 < best_local_s:
                       best_local_s=s2; best_f=f; best_a=a2
                # random factor occasionally
                if rng.random() < 0.25:
                    f=float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,0.35)))
                    a2=a.copy(); a2[l:r]*=f
                    s2=fast_score(a2)
                    if s2 < best_local_s: ...
                # refine if improved
                if best_f != 1.0:
                   a=best_a; s=best_local_s; improved=True
                   # fine search around best_f
                   fine_deadline=min(chunk_deadline, time+0.05)
                   for f in (best_f*0.9, best_f*0.95, best_f*1.05, best_f*1.1):
                       ...
        # after B done, update? we already update inside.
    return a
```

def v_population_search(baseline, n, B, deadline, seed):
    baseline = normalize_array(baseline)
    block_lengths = np.full(B, n//B); remainder...
    ids = np.repeat(np.arange(B), block_lengths)
    def eval_v(v): nonlocal not allowed? Top-level no closure; but we can make a top-level function `v_score(baseline, ids, v)` that returns fast_score(baseline*exp(clip(v))). No closure.
    v = np.zeros(B)
    best_v = v; best_s = v_score(...)
    pop = [(best_s, best_v.copy())]
    rng = default_rng
    for _ in range(pop_size-1):
       v = rng.normal(0, sigma_init, B); v-=v.mean(); score; append sorted
    sigma = sigma_init
    gen=0
    while time<deadline:
       gen +=1
       if not pop: break
       # select parent: if rng<0.5 best else random
       p = pop[0][1].copy() if ... else random
       if rng<0.3:
           # coordinate move
           b=rng.integers(B); p[b]+=rng.choice([-0.5,-0.2,0.2,0.5])
       else:
           p = p + sigma*rng.normal(size=B)
       p -= p.mean()
       p = np.clip(p, -4, 4)
       s = v_score(...)
       # update population: if better than worst or best
       if s < pop[-1][0] or s < pop[0][0]:
           if s < pop[0][0]: best_v=p; best_s=s
           if s < pop[-1][0]: pop.pop()
           # insert sorted: pop.append; sort using itemgetter
       # adapt sigma
       if s < pop[0][0]: sigma *=0.98; if sigma<0.05: sigma=0.05
       else: sigma *=1.02; cap 1.5
       # occasionally restart best with larger noise? if no improvement many gens
    return baseline * np.exp(np.clip(best_v,-4,4))
```

Detailed function implementations:

```
import numpy as np
import time
from operator import itemgetter

def next_pow2(m):
    if m <= 1: return 1
    return 1 << (m - 1).bit_length()

def normalize_array(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.array([1.0])
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = a.sum()
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-15:
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    a = a / s
    return a

def fast_score(a):
    a = normalize_array(a)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0: return np.inf
    need = 2*n - 1
    if n <= 128:
        c = np.convolve(a, a, mode='full')
        M = float(np.max(c))
    else:
        N = 1 << (need - 1).bit_length()
        fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
        c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N, ...)
        # irfft n parameter N default correct
        c = c[:need]
        M = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(M) or M < 0: M = 0.0
    return float(2.0 * n * M)
```
Check `np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, N)` returns length N. Good.

`make_beta(n, beta)`:
```
def make_beta_sequence(n, beta):
    a=np.ones(n)
    if n>1 and beta>0:
       k=np.arange(n-1, dtype=np.float64)
       ratios=(beta + k)/(k+1.0)
       a[1:]=np.cumprod(ratios)
    elif beta<=0:
       a=np.zeros(n); a[0]=1
    return a
```
For beta=1 ratios=1 -> ones. For beta=0.5 good.
But for beta=0.2 ratio k=0 0 -> all rest zero; need beta>0. Use min 0.05.

`make_sequence(n, kind, p1=0,p2=0)`:
Could avoid, generate directly in a function.

`generate_initials(n, seed)`:
```
def generate_initials(n, seed=1):
    arrs=[]
    i=np.arange(n,dtype=float)
    t=i/(n-1) if n>1 else np.zeros(1)
    base=make_beta_sequence(n,0.5)
    arrs.append(base)
    for beta in [0.45,0.5,0.55,0.6,0.7,0.8,1.0,0.35]:
       arrs.append(make_beta_sequence(n,beta))
    # tilts on base
    for tilt in [-0.3,-0.1,0.1,0.2,0.3,0.5,0.7]:
       a=base*(1.0+tilt*t)
       if np.min(a)>1e-12: arrs.append(a)
    # exp tilts
    for g in [-0.5,-0.2,0.1,0.3,0.6,1.0]:
       a=base*np.exp(g*t)
       arrs.append(a)
    # smooth cos
    for freq, amp in [(1,0.2),(1,0.5),(2,0.3),(3,0.3)]:
       a=base*(1.0+amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*freq*t + 0.7))
       if np.min(a)>1e-12: arrs.append(a)
    # power shifted
    for gamma in [0.5,1.0,2.0]:
       a=(i+gamma)**-0.5
       arrs.append(a)
    # linear
    for q in [0.2,0.5,0.8]:
       a=1.0 - q*t
       arrs.append(a)
    # two-level
    for tt in [0.2,1/3,0.4,0.6,0.8]:
       half=n//2
       a=np.ones(n); a[:half]=1+tt; a[half:]=1-tt
       arrs.append(a)
    # random smooth from base
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    for _ in range(4):
       f1=rng.integers(1,4); f2=rng.integers(2,7)
       amp1=float(rng.uniform(0.1,0.6)); amp2=float(rng.uniform(0.05,0.3))
       ph1=float(rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi)); ph2=float(rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi))
       a=base*(1+amp1*np.cos(2*np.pi*f1*t+ph1)+amp2*np.cos(2*np.pi*f2*t+ph2))
       if np.min(a)>1e-12: arrs.append(a)
    return arrs
```
Need avoid too many for n large; for n=2048 maybe 40 arrays, okay. For n=4096 maybe generate fewer? We can scan n up to 1024 initially; large use Taylor/selected.

`update_best_and_top` as above.

`block_refine_one` maybe inside `block_refine`.

Implement `block_refine`:
```
def block_refine(a, deadline, seed, B_list=None, max_chunks=3):
    a=normalize_array(a); n=a.size
    s=fast_score(a)
    if B_list is None: ...
    B_list = [B for B in B_list if B > 0]
    if not B_list: return a
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    now=time.monotonic()
    total=deadline-now
    if total<1: return a
    time_per=max(2.0, total/len(B_list))
    for B in B_list:
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
        block_size=max(1, n//B)
        Bnow=max(1, n//block_size)
        chunk_deadline=min(deadline, time.monotonic()+time_per)
        improved=True
        factors=(0.7,0.85,1.15,1.3)
        while improved and time.monotonic() < chunk_deadline:
            improved=False
            order=rng.permutation(Bnow)
            for b in order:
                if time.monotonic() >= chunk_deadline: break
                l=b*block_size; r=min(n,l+block_size)
                best_s=s; best_f=1.0; best_a=None
                for f in factors:
                    a2=a.copy(); a2[l:r]*=f
                    s2=fast_score(a2)
                    if s2 < best_s:
                        best_s=s2; best_f=f; best_a=a2
                if rng.random() < 0.2:
                    f=float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0,0.4)))
                    a2=a.copy(); a2[l:r]*=f
                    s2=fast_score(a2)
                    if s2 < best_s:
                        best_s=s2; best_f=f; best_a=a2
                if best_f != 1.0 and best_a is not None:
                    # fine search
                    a=best_a; s=best_s; improved=True
                    fine_factors=(best_f*0.9, best_f*0.95, best_f*1.05, best_f*1.1)
                    for f in fine_factors:
                        if time.monotonic() >= chunk_deadline: break
                        a2=a.copy(); a2[l:r]*=f
                        s2=fast_score(a2)
                        if s2 < s:
                            a=a2; s=s2
                    # maybe reset local chunk if good? not
        # after B, if s finite, nothing
    return a
```
Potential issue: If a block improves, we don't re-evaluate other factors for same block after fine; okay.
`time_per` constant, if first B no improvement quickly, next begins before time_per? It will immediately go next, not wait. Good. If first B improves until chunk dead, moves next with remaining. Fine.

`v_score` top-level:
```
def v_score(baseline, ids, v):
    e=np.exp(np.clip(v, -4.0, 4.0))
    a=baseline * e[ids]
    return fast_score(a), baseline * e[ids]
```
But returning both allocates a twice? We can compute a once. Function needs just score? We need final a. Do:
```
def apply_v(baseline, ids, v):
    e=np.exp(np.clip(v,-4,4))
    return baseline * e[ids]

def v_score(baseline, ids, v):
    return fast_score(apply_v(baseline,ids,v))
```
But repeated apply in population. For each offspring, compute a and score.

`v_population_search`:
```
def v_population_search(baseline, n, B, deadline, seed):
    baseline=normalize_array(baseline)
    if n < B: B=n
    lengths=np.full(B, n//B, dtype=int)
    rem=n - B*(n//B)
    if rem>0:
        idx=rng? but rng not yet; we can distribute first rem: lengths[:rem]+=1
    ids=np.repeat(np.arange(B), lengths)
    rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
    best_v=np.zeros(B)
    best_a=baseline.copy()
    best_s=fast_score(best_a)
    sigma=0.8
    pop=[(best_s,best_v.copy())]
    for _ in range(9):
       v=rng.normal(0.0, sigma, B)
       v-=v.mean()
       v=np.clip(v,-4,4)
       a=baseline*np.exp(v[ids])
       s=fast_score(a)
       if s < pop[-1][0] or len(pop)<10:
          pop.append((s,v))
          pop.sort(key=itemgetter(0))
          if len(pop)>10: pop.pop()
          if s<best_s: best_s=s; best_v=v.copy(); best_a=a.copy()
    no_improve=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       if not pop: break
       # parent selection
       if rng.random() < 0.5:
          p=pop[0][1].copy()
       else:
          p=rng.choice(len(pop))? Use rng.integers
          p=pop[int(rng.integers(0,len(pop)))][1].copy()

          b=int(rng.integers(0,B))
          p[b] += float(rng.choice(np.array([-0.6,-0.3,0.3,0.6])))
       else:
          p = p + sigma * rng.normal(0.0,1.0,B)
       p -= p.mean()
       p = np.clip(p,-4,4)
       a=baseline*np.exp(p[ids])
       s=fast_score(a)
       if s < pop[0][0] or s < pop[-1][0]:
           if s < best_s:
               best_s=s; best_v=p.copy(); best_a=a.copy()
           if s < pop[-1][0]: pop.pop()
           pop.append((s,p))
           pop.sort(key=itemgetter(0))
           if len(pop)>10: pop.pop()
           no_improve=0
           sigma=max(0.05, sigma*0.97)
       else:
           no_improve+=1
           if no_improve > 50:
               sigma=min(1.5, sigma*1.1)
               if no_improve > 200:
                   no_improve=0
                   # reset one to random around best with larger sigma
                   v=pop[0][1] + 2.0*rng.normal(0,1,B); v-=v.mean(); clip
                   ... evaluate replace worst maybe
    return best_a
```

Modify `normalize_array`: no cap 1000 before sum, but final values <=1.
```
def normalize_array(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0: return np.array([1.0])
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a) & (a > 0.0), a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-15:
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    a = a / s
    # due to previous zeros maybe sum exactly 1
    return a
```
If all zero, returns ones (score 2). Good.

Pseudo code for v_pop:
```
def v_population_search(baseline, n, B, deadline, seed):
    baseline = normalize_array(baseline)
    B = max(1, min(B, n))
    lengths = np.full(B, n // B, dtype=int)
    rem = n - B * (n // B)
    if rem:
        lengths[:rem] += 1
    ids = np.repeat(np.arange(B), lengths)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    best_v = np.zeros(B)
    best_a = baseline.copy()
    best_s = fast_score(best_a)
    pop = [(best_s, best_v.copy())]
    sigma = 0.7
    for _ in range(12):
        v = rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=B)
        v -= v.mean()
        v = np.clip(v, -4.0, 4.0)
        a = baseline * np.exp(v[ids])
        s = fast_score(a)
        if len(pop) < 12 or s < pop[-1][0]:
            pop.append((s, v))
            pop.sort(key=itemgetter(0))
            if len(pop) > 12: pop.pop()
            if s < best_s:
                best_s=s; best_v=v.copy(); best_a=a.copy()
    no_improve=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if not pop: break
        if rng.random() < 0.5:
            p = pop[0][1].copy()
        else:
            idx = int(rng.integers(0, len(pop)))
            p = pop[idx][1].copy()
        if rng.random() < 0.25:
            b = int(rng.integers(0, B))
            delta = float(rng.normal(0.0, 0.3))
            p[b] += delta
        else:
            p = p + sigma * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, size=B)
        p -= p.mean()
        p = np.clip(p, -4.0, 4.0)
        a = baseline * np.exp(p[ids])
        s = fast_score(a)
        if s < pop[0][0] or s < pop[-1][0]:
            if s < best_s:
                best_s = s; best_v = p.copy(); best_a = a.copy()
            if s < pop[-1][0]:
                pop.pop()
            pop.append((s, p))
            pop.sort(key=itemgetter(0))
            if len(pop) > 12: pop.pop()
            no_improve = 0
            sigma = max(0.03, sigma * 0.98)
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve > 40:
                sigma = min(1.6, sigma * 1.15)
                if no_improve > 200:
                    no_improve = 0
                    # random restart around best
                    v = pop[0][1] + 1.5 * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, size=B)
                    v -= v.mean(); v=np.clip(v,-4,4)
                    a=baseline*np.exp(v[ids]); s=fast_score(a)
                    if s < pop[-1][0]:
                       ...
    return best_a, best_s
```
Note: If s equals pop[-1][0] maybe replace? Use <=.

`improve_candidate` code:
```
def improve_candidate(best, top, a, deadline, seed):
    if a is None: return
    a=normalize_array(a)
    local_s=fast_score(a)
    update_best_and_top(best, top, a)
    n=a.size
    if n < 2 or deadline - time.monotonic() < 5: return
    t=np.arange(n, dtype=float)
    if n>1: t=t/(n-1)
    local_best=a; local_best_s=local_s
    for tilt in (-0.3,-0.15,0.15,0.3,0.5,0.7,-0.5):
       aa=a*(1+tilt*t)
       s=fast_score(aa)
       update_best_and_top(best, top, aa)
       if s < local_best_s:
          local_best=aa; local_best_s=s
    # global best same n
    if best[0] is not None and best[0].size==n and best[1] < local_best_s:
       local_best = normalize_array(best[0]); local_best_s=best[1]
    local_best = block_refine(local_best, deadline, seed)[0]
    local_best_s = fast_score(local_best) # maybe redundant
    update_best_and_top(best, top, local_best)
    if time.monotonic() < deadline:
       # vsearch B list depending n and time
       v_list = [8,16] if n <= 1024 else [8,16]
       remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
       for i,B in enumerate(v_list):
          if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
          chunk = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + max(5.0, remaining/(len(v_list)-i)))
          va, vs = v_population_search(local_best, n, B, chunk, seed + 1000*i)
          if vs < local_best_s:
              local_best=va; local_best_s=vs
          update_best_and_top(best, top, va)
    # final block on local if time
    if time.monotonic() < deadline:
       local_best = block_refine(local_best, deadline, seed+7777, choose_B_list(n))[0]
       update_best_and_top(best, top, local_best)
```

`upsample_sequence`:
```
def upsample_sequence(old, n_new):
    old=normalize_array(old)
    if old.size == n_new: return old
    if old.size <= 1: return np.full(n_new, 1.0)
    x_old=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,old.size)
    x_new=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n_new)
    y=np.interp(x_new, x_old, old)
    y=np.clip(y,0.0,None)
    s=y.sum()
    if s<=0: return make_beta_sequence(n_new,0.5)
    return y/s
```

`make_beta_sequence` for n=0? ensure.

Use [0.45,0.5,0.55,0.6,0.7,0.8,1.0]. Shifted gamma covers flatter? gamma<1 not. Fine.

```python
"""
Search for non-negative step-function heights minimizing
    2*n*max(np.convolve(a,a)) / (sum(a)**2).

Strategy:
- Use fast FFT evaluations of the exact score.
- Start from several structured one-sided / smooth oscillatory seeds,
  especially the finite Taylor coefficients of (1-x)^(-1/2), which already
  approaches ~pi/2 asymptotically.
- Refine candidates by:
    * small global tilts / oscillations,
    * multiplicative block coordinate search,
    * low-dimensional population search over block log-magnitudes.
- Keep a small exact-validated top list at the end.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
from operator import itemgetter


def normalize_array(a):
    """Clip to non-negative finite reals and normalize sum to 1."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return np.array([1.0], dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, None)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-15:
        return np.ones(a.size, dtype=np.float64)
    return a / s


def fast_score(a):
    """
    Internally fast evaluation of the objective.
    Equivalent to evaluate_sequence for finite non-negative inputs,
    because the objective is scale invariant.
    """
    a = normalize_array(a)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")

    if n <= 256:
        c = np.convolve(a, a, mode="full")
        m = float(np.max(c))
    else:
        need = 2 * n - 1
        N = 1 << (need - 1).bit_length()
        fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
        c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, N)[:need]
        m = float(np.max(c))

    if not np.isfinite(m) or m < 0.0:
        m = 0.0
    return float(2.0 * n * m)


def make_beta_sequence(n, beta):
    """
    Coefficients of (1-x)^(-beta), truncated to length n.
    beta=0.5 gives the central-binomial square-root-geometric sequence.
    """
    n = int(max(1, n))
    a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if n <= 1 or beta <= 0.0:
        if beta <= 0.0:
            a = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
            a[0] = 1.0
        return a

    k = np.arange(n - 1, dtype=np.float64)
    ratios = (beta + k) / (k + 1.0)
    a[1:] = np.cumprod(ratios)
    return a


def upsample_sequence(old, n_new):
    """Linearly interpolate a positive sequence to a new length."""
    old = normalize_array(old)
    n_new = int(max(1, n_new))
    if old.size == n_new:
        return old
    if old.size <= 1:
        return np.ones(n_new, dtype=np.float64)

    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, old.size)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n_new)
    y = np.interp(x_new, x_old, old)
    y = np.clip(y, 0.0, None)
    s = float(y.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 1e-15:
        return make_beta_sequence(n_new, 0.5)
    return y / s


def generate_initials(n, seed=1):
    """
    Produce a diverse set of positive seed sequences of length n.
    """
    n = int(max(2, n))
    i = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    t = i / float(n - 1)

    arrs = []
    base = make_beta_sequence(n, 0.5)
    arrs.append(base)

    # Pure beta-family: beta around 1/2 is strongest asymptotically.
    for beta in (0.45, 0.5, 0.55, 0.6, 0.7, 0.8, 1.0):
        arrs.append(make_beta_sequence(n, beta))

    # Linear/exponential tilts on the Taylor base: move mass toward the end.
    for tilt in (-0.3, -0.1, 0.1, 0.2, 0.3, 0.5, 0.7):
        a = base * (1.0 + tilt * t)
        if np.min(a) > 1e-12:
            arrs.append(a)

    for g in (-0.5, -0.2, 0.1, 0.3, 0.6, 1.0):
        a = base * np.exp(g * t)
        if np.min(a) > 1e-12:
            arrs.append(a)

    # Shifting the endpoint can be useful at finite n.
    for gamma in (0.5, 1.0, 2.0):
        arrs.append((i + gamma) ** (-0.5))

    # Smooth positive periodic perturbations.
    for freq, amp in ((1, 0.2), (1, 0.5), (2, 0.3), (3, 0.3)):
        a = base * (1.0 + amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * freq * t + 0.7))
        if np.min(a) > 1e-12:
            arrs.append(a)

    # Mild linear densities.
    for q in (0.2, 0.5, 0.8):
        arrs.append(1.0 - q * t)

    # Two-level block construction.
    half = n // 2
    for tt in (0.2, 1.0 / 3.0, 0.4, 0.6, 0.8):
        a = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
        a[:half] = 1.0 + tt
        a[half:] = 1.0 - tt
        arrs.append(a)

    # A few random low-frequency smooth multipliers.
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    for _ in range(5):
        f1 = int(rng.integers(1, 4))
        f2 = int(rng.integers(2, 7))
        a1 = float(rng.uniform(0.1, 0.6))
        a2 = float(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.3))
        p1 = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        p2 = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        a = base * (
            1.0
            + a1 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * f1 * t + p1)
            + a2 * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * f2 * t + p2)
        )
        if np.min(a) > 1e-12:
            arrs.append(a)

    return arrs


def update_best_and_top(best, top, a):
    """
    best: [array, score]
    top: list of (score, array), sorted ascending by score, kept <= 5.
    """
    if a is None:
        return
    a = normalize_array(a)
    if a.size == 0:
        return
    s = fast_score(a)
    if not np.isfinite(s):
        return

    if s < best[1]:
        best[0] = a.copy()
        best[1] = s
        top.append((s, a.copy()))
        top.sort(key=itemgetter(0))
        if len(top) > 5:
            top.pop()
    else:
        if len(top) < 5 or s < top[-1][0]:
            top.append((s, a.copy()))
            top.sort(key=itemgetter(0))
            if len(top) > 5:
                top.pop()


def choose_B_list(n):
    if n <= 256:
        return [8, 16, 32]
    if n <= 1024:
        return [16, 32, 64]
    if n <= 4096:
        return [16, 32, 64, 128]
    return [16, 32, 64, 128]


def block_refine(a, deadline, seed=1, B_list=None):
    """
    Multiplicative block coordinate ascent on score.
    Returns (best_array, best_score).
    """
    a = normalize_array(a)
    n = a.size
    s = fast_score(a)

    if B_list is None:
        B_list = choose_B_list(n)
    B_list = [int(max(1, B)) for B in B_list if B > 0]
    if not B_list or n < 2:
        return a, s

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    total_deadline = float(max(0.0, deadline))
    if time.monotonic() >= total_deadline:
        return a, s

    now = time.monotonic()
    total_time = max(1.0, total_deadline - now)
    time_per = max(2.0, total_time / float(len(B_list)))

    for B in B_list:
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now >= total_deadline:
            break
        chunk_deadline = min(total_deadline, now + time_per)

        block_size = max(1, n // B)
        Bnow = max(1, n // block_size)

        improved = True
        while improved and time.monotonic() < chunk_deadline:
            improved = False
            order = rng.permutation(Bnow)

            for b in order:
                if time.monotonic() >= chunk_deadline:
                    break

                l = int(b * block_size)
                r = min(n, l + block_size)

                best_s = s
                best_f = 1.0
                best_a = None

                # Static factors around 1.
                for f in (0.7, 0.85, 1.15, 1.3):
                    a2 = a.copy()
                    a2[l:r] *= f
                    s2 = fast_score(a2)
                    if s2 < best_s:
                        best_s = s2
                        best_f = f
                        best_a = a2

                # Occasionally try a random multiplicative step.
                if rng.random() < 0.25:
                    f = float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.4)))
                    a2 = a.copy()
                    a2[l:r] *= f
                    s2 = fast_score(a2)
                    if s2 < best_s:
                        best_s = s2
                        best_f = f
                        best_a = a2

                if best_f != 1.0 and best_a is not None:
                    a = normalize_array(best_a)
                    s = best_s
                    improved = True

                    # Local tightening around accepted factor.
                    for f in (best_f * 0.9, best_f * 0.95, best_f * 1.05, best_f * 1.1):
                        if time.monotonic() >= chunk_deadline:
                            break
                        a2 = a.copy()
                        a2[l:r] *= f
                        s2 = fast_score(a2)
                        if s2 < s:
                            a = normalize_array(a2)
                            s = s2

    return a, s


def v_population_search(baseline, n, B, deadline, seed=1):
    """
    Low-dimensional population search over block log-magnitudes:
        a_i = baseline_i * exp(v_[block(i)]).
    Returns (best_array, best_score).
    """
    baseline = normalize_array(baseline)
    n = int(baseline.size)
    B = int(max(1, min(B, n)))

    lengths = np.full(B, n // B, dtype=int)
    rem = n - B * (n // B)
    if rem > 0:
        lengths[:rem] += 1
    ids = np.repeat(np.arange(B), lengths)

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    best_v = np.zeros(B, dtype=np.float64)
    best_a = baseline.copy()
    best_s = fast_score(best_a)

    pop = [(best_s, best_v.copy())]
    sigma = 0.7
    pop_size = 12

    for _ in range(pop_size):
        v = rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=B)
        v -= float(v.mean())
        v = np.clip(v, -4.0, 4.0)
        a = baseline * np.exp(v[ids])
        s = fast_score(a)

        if len(pop) < pop_size or s < pop[-1][0]:
            pop.append((s, v))
            pop.sort(key=itemgetter(0))
            if len(pop) > pop_size:
                pop.pop()
            if s < best_s:
                best_s = s
                best_v = v.copy()
                best_a = a.copy()

    no_improve = 0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if not pop:
            break

        if rng.random() < 0.5:
            p = pop[0][1].copy()
        else:
            idx = int(rng.integers(0, len(pop)))
            p = pop[idx][1].copy()

        if rng.random() < 0.25:
            b = int(rng.integers(0, B))
            p[b] += float(rng.normal(0.0, 0.3))
        else:
            p = p + sigma * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, size=B)

        p -= float(p.mean())
        p = np.clip(p, -4.0, 4.0)

        a = baseline * np.exp(p[ids])
        s = fast_score(a)

        if s < pop[0][0] or s < pop[-1][0]:
            if s < best_s:
                best_s = s
                best_v = p.copy()
                best_a = a.copy()

            if s < pop[-1][0]:
                pop.pop()
            pop.append((s, p))
            pop.sort(key=itemgetter(0))
            if len(pop) > pop_size:
                pop.pop()

            no_improve = 0
            sigma = max(0.03, sigma * 0.98)
        else:
            no_improve += 1
            if no_improve > 40:
                sigma = min(1.6, sigma * 1.15)

                if no_improve > 200:
                    no_improve = 0
                    v = pop[0][1] + 1.5 * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, size=B)
                    v -= float(v.mean())
                    v = np.clip(v, -4.0, 4.0)
                    a2 = baseline * np.exp(v[ids])
                    s2 = fast_score(a2)

                    if s2 < pop[-1][0]:
                        pop.pop()
                        pop.append((s2, v))
                        pop.sort(key=itemgetter(0))
                        if len(pop) > pop_size:
                            pop.pop()
                        if s2 < best_s:
                            best_s = s2
                            best_v = v.copy()
                            best_a = a2.copy()

    return best_a, best_s


def improve_candidate(best, top, a, deadline, seed=1):
    """
    Improve one candidate with tilts, block refine, and low-dimensional search.
    """
    if a is None:
        return
    a = normalize_array(a)
    local_s = fast_score(a)
    update_best_and_top(best, top, a)

    n = a.size
    now = time.monotonic()
    if n < 2 or now >= deadline:
        return

    t = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(n - 1)

    local_best = a.copy()
    local_best_s = local_s

    # Quick global trend moves.
    for tilt in (-0.5, -0.3, -0.15, 0.15, 0.3, 0.5, 0.7):
        aa = a * (1.0 + tilt * t)
        s = fast_score(aa)
        update_best_and_top(best, top, aa)
        if s < local_best_s:
            local_best = aa.copy()
            local_best_s = s

    # If some candidate of the same length is better, use it as local seed.
    if best[0] is not None and best[0].size == n and best[1] < local_best_s:
        local_best = normalize_array(best[0])
        local_best_s = best[1]

    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining > 10:
        block_deadline = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + max(10.0, 0.6 * remaining))
        local_best, local_best_s = block_refine(
            local_best, block_deadline, seed, choose_B_list(n)
        )
        update_best_and_top(best, top, local_best)

    remaining = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remaining > 20:
        v_list = [8, 16] if n <= 2048 else [8]
        for j, B in enumerate(v_list):
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
                break
            chunk = min(deadline, time.monotonic() + max(10.0, remaining / float(len(v_list) - j)))
            va, vs = v_population_search(local_best, n, B, chunk, seed + 1000 * (j + 1))
            if vs < local_best_s:
                local_best = va
                local_best_s = vs
            update_best_and_top(best, top, va)

    # Final polish on the locally best array.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline:
        local_best, local_best_s = block_refine(
            local_best, deadline, seed + 7777, choose_B_list(n)
        )
        update_best_and_top(best, top, local_best)


def propose_candidate(budget_s=1500, **kwargs):
    """
    Main entry point. Searches for a low-scoring non-negative sequence.
    """
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(20.0, float(budget_s) - 30.0)

    best = [None, float("inf")]
    top = []

    # Use supplied parent if available.
    try:
        parent = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
        if parent.size > 0:
            update_best_and_top(best, top, parent)
            print(f"[init] parent len={parent.size} score={best[1]:.6f}")
    except Exception:
        pass

    # Structured scan over moderate lengths.
    scan_deadline = min(deadline, start + 30.0)
    for n in (64, 128, 256, 512, 1024):
        if time.monotonic() > scan_deadline:
            break
        for arr in generate_initials(n, seed=100 + n):
            update_best_and_top(best, top, arr)
            if time.monotonic() > scan_deadline:
                break

    if best[0] is None:
        fb = make_beta_sequence(256, 0.5)
        best[0] = fb
        best[1] = fast_score(fb)
        top.append((best[1], fb))

    print(f"[scan] best len={best[0].size if best[0] is not None else 0} score={best[1]:.6f} time={time.monotonic()-start:.1f}s")

    # Refine the top small/moderate candidates.
    refine_deadline = min(deadline, start + 200.0)
    for it in top[:3]:
        if time.monotonic() >= refine_deadline:
            break
        arr = it[1]
        chunk = min(refine_deadline, time.monotonic() + 70.0)
        improve_candidate(best, top, arr, chunk, seed=5000 + arr.size)

    print(f"[mid] best len={best[0].size if best[0] is not None else 0} score={best[1]:.6f} time={time.monotonic()-start:.1f}s")

    # Multi-resolution: upscale a good small candidate, and also use large Taylor base.
    remain = deadline - time.monotonic()
    if remain > 150:
        if remain > 1200:
            n_large = 8192
        elif remain > 600:
            n_large = 4096
        else:
            n_large = 2048

        large_candidates = []

        if best[0] is not None and best[0].size < n_large:
            large_candidates.append(upsample_sequence(best[0], n_large))

        if len(top) > 1 and top[1][1] is not None and top[1][1].size < n_large:
            large_candidates.append(upsample_sequence(top[1][1], n_large))

        large_candidates.append(make_beta_sequence(n_large, 0.5))

        # Optionally add a slightly tilted Taylor base.
        tb = make_beta_sequence(n_large, 0.5)
        tt = np.arange(n_large, dtype=np.float64) / float(n_large - 1)
        tilted = tb * (1.0 + 0.25 * tt)
        if np.min(tilted) > 1e-12:
            large_candidates.append(tilted)

        for j, cand in enumerate(large_candidates[:3]):
            if time.monotonic() >= deadline - 30:
                break
            improve_candidate(best, top, cand, deadline - 30, seed=9000 + n_large + 100 * j)

    print(f"[large] best len={best[0].size if best[0] is not None else 0} score={best[1]:.6f} time={time.monotonic()-start:.1f}s")

    # Final polish on current best if time remains.
    if best[0] is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 20:
        improve_candidate(best, top, best[0], deadline - 20, seed=20000 + best[0].size)

    print(f"[final-internal] best len={best[0].size if best[0] is not None else 0} score={best[1]:.6f} time={time.monotonic()-start:.1f}s")

    # Exact verification of a few top candidates.
    final_seq = None
    final_exact = float("inf")
    eval_list = []
    for sc, arr in top[:3]:
        if arr is not None and arr.size <= 10000:
            eval_list.append((sc, arr))
        if len(eval_list) >= 3:
            break

    if not eval_list and best[0] is not None:
        eval_list.append((best[1], best[0]))

    for sc, arr in eval_list:
        try:
            seq = [float(x) for x in arr]
            es = evaluate_sequence(seq)
            es = float(es)
            if np.isfinite(es) and es < final_exact:
                final_exact = es
                final_seq = seq
        except Exception:
            pass

    if final_seq is None and best[0] is not None:
        final_seq = [float(x) for x in best[0]]
    else:
        final_seq = [float(x) for x in best[0]]

    print(f"[return] len={len(final_seq)} exact={final_exact if np.isfinite(final_exact) else 0}")
    return final_seq
```
